That reads `OPERANT_ENV_FILE`, or `.env` in the working directory, and overwrites any changed variable in the process env. `/connect` pins go to `~/.operant/.env` (`crates/operant-cli/src/env_store.rs:41`, `:95`). A pin in the working-directory `.env` is therefore never updated by `/connect`. This matches "any message" only for messages that go through the gateway.
- **Not determined:** a clean run against aroute did not return within 90 s (the `timeout 90` killed it). The cause was not investigated.

Plan:

1. **Show provenance (ENV-1).** `operant config show` and the TUI header mark each value that came from an env var, and the loader warns once per overridden key. Verify: the stale-env repro prints the marker and one warning; a clean env prints neither.
2. **Decide precedence (owner decision).** (a) Keep env-over-TOML and add warnings only. (b) TOML wins; env fills only keys the TOML leaves unset. (c) Drop bare `HERMES_MODEL`, keep `OPERANT_*` names only. Recommendation: (a) now, and revisit (b) once the phase 1 warnings have been seen in use.
3. **Stop env proxies from overriding config (ENV-2).** Call `no_proxy()` on the shared client builders unless `[proxy]` names a proxy, or honor environment proxies only under `[proxy] scope = "environment"`, which the `ProxyScope` enum already has. Verify: a test binds a TCP listener on an ephemeral port, sets `HTTPS_PROXY` to it, and asserts zero accepted connections. The stale-proxy repro no longer prints `tunnel error`.
4. **Retire the dead schema (ENV-3).** Confirm `Config::load_or_init` has no callers, then delete it, or warn when `~/.operant/config.toml` sits beside `operant.toml`. Verify: `operant config show` reads only `operant.toml`, and no test depends on the `config.toml` path.
5. **One `.env` (ENV-4).** Point `reload_dotenv` at `operant_env_path()` unless `OPERANT_ENV_FILE` is set, or warn when a working-directory `.env` shadows it. Verify: a gateway turn with a working-directory `.env` containing `OPENAI_BASE_URL` logs the shadow warning and does not change the endpoint.
6. **Document the precedence** (env, then `.env` pins, then `operant.toml`) in `operant.example.toml` and the README troubleshooting section, with the check below.

**Status (2026-10-21):** steps 1, 3, 4, 5, 6 are DONE and pushed — RT-130 (ENV-1 provenance), RT-132 (ENV-3 dead `config.toml` warning, plus the ENV-1 marker in `env_passthrough`), RT-133 (ENV-4 `config.toml` shadow), RT-134 (ENV-2 env-proxy opt-in, inverted test probes for a *new* builder because the shared one is built before `[proxy]` is applied), and the precedence text in `operant.example.toml` + `README.md` + `docs/CHANGELOG.md`. **Step 2 (precedence) is OPEN and is an owner decision** — nothing above changes precedence. Live E2E with the deployed binary (iter-1118): `OPERANT_CONFIG_DIR=<tmp> OPENAI_BASE_URL=… operant config show` names the sibling `config.toml` it did not read, names the file it did, and the trailer lists `OPENAI_BASE_URL`; a clean shell prints no trailer.

Workaround (no code change), run in the user's terminal, names only:

```sh
env | grep -E '^(OPENAI_|HERMES_|OPERANT_|HTTPS?_PROXY|ALL_PROXY|MODEL|PROVIDER|PORT)' | sed 's/=.*/=…/'
env -u OPENAI_BASE_URL -u HERMES_MODEL -u HTTPS_PROXY -u HTTP_PROXY -u ALL_PROXY operant …
```

- [x] **RT-130 (S) — a shell variable overrode `operant.toml` with no trace, which is why the reported "ignores the configured model" could not be diagnosed.** Step 1 of the plan above (ENV-1 provenance), FIXED iter-1115: `read_env` now records every variable it actually applies and warns **once per key per process** ("environment variable overrides the configured value; unset it to use the config file"), and `operant config show` appends the overridden key names below the YAML — the effective values stay visible, and the reader is told which of them did not come from the file. Precedence is UNCHANGED (env still wins); this is provenance, not a policy change, because the plan's step 2 (precedence) is an owner decision and step 3 (proxy policy) is not implemented. Proof: `an_env_override_is_recorded_and_visible` (sets `HERMES_MODEL`, applies the overrides, asserts both that the env value wins AND that the key is reported — mutation-checked: dropping the `record_env_override` call fails the second assertion, keeping it while changing precedence fails the first); end to end `HERMES_MODEL=probe operant config show | tail -4` prints `# HERMES_MODEL` and a clean env prints no trailer (note the trailer sits AFTER the YAML dump — `head` truncates it, which is how a first check mistook it for missing). Residual: the trailer names KEYS, not per-line markers in the YAML (serde cannot attach comments), and the TUI header does not yet show it — the loader warning reaches the TUI log instead.

- [x] **RT-131 (S) — provider traffic followed `HTTP(S)_PROXY`/`ALL_PROXY`, so a stale variable from another tool silently sent every model request to a dead port.** Step 3 of the plan above (ENV-2), FIXED iter-1116: both provider HTTP clients (`OpenAIClient::new` and `stream_http_client`, `crates/operant-core/src/client.rs`) now call `.no_proxy()`, so env proxies can no longer redirect model traffic. Proof: `env_proxy_is_ignored_by_the_provider_client` binds a capture listener as the "stale proxy", points every proxy variable (upper and lower case) at it, and drives the REAL client (`embeddings`) — a loopback request proves direct traffic still works, and a TEST-NET-1 request proves the proxy is not consulted (loopback alone cannot discriminate: reqwest bypasses proxies for it even without `no_proxy()`, which is why the first version of this test passed under mutation). Mutation-checked: dropping `.no_proxy()` from `new()` makes the capture listener record 1 connection and the test fails (`left: 1, right: 0`). Residual (checked against the code, not the intent): a `[proxy]` table DOES exist in `operant-config`'s schema with `ProxyConfig::apply_to_reqwest_builder` / `apply_runtime_proxy_to_builder`, but that function has NO caller outside `proxy.rs`, so the provider clients never read it — the environment was the only proxy path reaching model traffic, and after this fix there is none. The live `~/.operant/operant.toml` carries no `[proxy]` table, so no current configuration loses working behavior. An operator who needs a proxy requires two follow-ups: plumb `[proxy]` into `ClientConfig` and decide whether `scope = "environment"` should proxy provider traffic (`should_apply_to_service` currently returns false for it), and only then relax `.no_proxy()`. The 15s wall time of the test is the connect attempt to TEST-NET-1, stable across runs.

- [x] **RT-132 (S) — `~/.operant/config.toml` sits in no load path, so editing it changes nothing and says nothing.** Step 4 of the plan above (ENV-3), FIXED iter-1117: `load_app_config` (`crates/operant-core/src/config.rs`) names a `config.toml` beside the live file once per process (`warning: … is not read — the live config is …`). The audit's "retire the dead schema" alternative was rejected: `operant-config`'s `Config::load_or_init` IS unreferenced outside its own tests, but the crate also carries the schema the live loader deserializes — deleting it would mean deleting the config types. Proof: `config_toml_beside_operant_toml_is_reported_as_ignored` (the helper names the dead file and never the chosen one). Residual: the warning only fires when a config file was actually chosen (`source` is `Some`), so a run on pure defaults with a stray `config.toml` stays silent — that run has no `operant.toml` to compare against. The live E2E (iter-1118) caught the first version naming a `config.toml` under `HERMES_HOME` while the loader had read `~/.operant`: the sibling is now derived from the chosen file's own directory, so `OPERANT_CONFIG_DIR`, the cwd candidate and the home default all agree. Also note `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/operant/config.toml` IS in `default_config_paths`; only the `~/.operant/config.toml` sibling is dead.
- [x] **RT-133 (S) — a working-directory `.env` loses to the home `.env` silently, so pins edited there never apply.** Step 5 of the plan above (ENV-4), FIXED iter-1117 — with a correction to the finding: the audit said the reload reads `OPERANT_ENV_FILE` "or `.env` in the working directory", but `dotenv_path` already prefers `<operant home>/.env` (`crates/operant-core/src/env_passthrough.rs`) and only falls back to the cwd file when the home file is absent, so `/connect` pins were already being read. The real gap was the silence when both files exist. `reload_dotenv` now warns once per process, naming both files. Proof: `a_working_directory_dotenv_shadowed_by_the_home_file_is_named`. Residual: one warning per PROCESS at the first reload, not per gateway turn; if the files appear mid-run the warning waits for the next process start.

## OPEN — Red-team: Holon seam, agentic-core seat and TUI (2026-10-19)

Found by a read-only red-team of the Holon seam (`crates/operant-os/`, the Holon-side branches of `operant-cli/src/cmd_org.rs`) and of the agentic-core and gateway seat (`operant-core/src/gateway_session.rs`, `gateway_runner.rs`, `cronjobs/scheduler.rs`, `memory_provider.rs`). Each row was checked against its code, and rows marked "read" were not run. The TUI interaction layer (`crates/operant-cli/src/tui/`) is covered by the `TUI-` rows at the end of this section. Dedupe: the rows below are new unless marked. `OS-` = Holon seam, `GW-` = agentic-core and gateway seat, `TUI-` = TUI interaction layer. The G-1 defect in `docs/plans/2026-10-19-org-and-tui-foundation-ratified-decisions-red-team-findings-.md` is the same as GW-9. M = user-visible or safety-relevant; H = a security boundary, data loss, or org mode silently not doing what it claims; L = hygiene or drift.

- [x] **OS-1 (H) — the body can read the supervisor's environment.** Fixed iter-967 (AD-006): `harden_supervisor()` clears `PR_SET_DUMPABLE` before every body spawn, so a same-uid body cannot read `/proc/<supervisor>/environ`. Test `a_body_cannot_read_its_supervisors_environment` (red with the call replaced by `true`). Off Linux a warning is logged per spawn, and nothing is hidden there. `run_body` runs `sh -c` as a same-uid child of the supervisor and calls `env_clear()` (`crates/operant-os/src/supervisor.rs:80-84, 101`). The body can read `/proc/$PPID/environ` and recover every variable that was removed. Reproduced only on the tool shell's own parent process, not on a supervisor. What the supervisor's environment holds depends on how it was started, so the impact is conditional. Dedupe: O-07 closed the environment-variable path only (`docs/agentic-os/AUDIT-2026-10-15.md`).
- [x] **OS-2 (H) — the chair keys are reachable from an act body.** Fixed iter-967 for the implicit path (AD-006): every body's `HOLON_CHAIR_KEY` is pinned to a value no chair digest matches (`body_env`; test `a_body_is_pinned_to_no_chair_key`, red without the pin), so a body that unsets its run token resolves to `anonymous`. **Residual, recorded in AD-006:** a same-uid body can still read the operator's key file explicitly. Closing that needs act bodies under a separate uid, the O-11 runner isolation. `ENV_BASELINE` passes `HOME` (`supervisor.rs:21-23`), and `~/.config/holon/keys/*.chair` is mode 600 owned by the operator. An act body can run `holon run finish` as chair and bypass policy and approval. Read; the holon key lookup was not exercised. Dedupe: O-11 covers only the isolated path.
- [x] **OS-3 (H) — enforcement fails open when `policy.json` is missing, with no log.** Fixed iter-967 (AD-007): a Holon run with no `policy.json` is refused at agent start (`policy_in`, `enforcement.rs`; test `a_holon_run_without_a_policy_document_is_refused_not_unfenced`, red with the old `None` return). `run_policy()` returns `None` when the file is absent (`crates/operant-os/src/enforcement.rs:36-42`). The `Some`-only log in `operant-cli/src/main.rs` then records nothing, and the native `holon_*` tools stay available. Read. The comment calls it "the same no-op", so this is a design gap, not an accident.
- [x] **OS-4 (H) — `holon_skill_invoke` runs in observe posture and writes the org ledger.** Fixed iter-963: added to `OBSERVE_HOLON_DENY` (`operant-os/src/policy.rs`; test `observe_posture_denies_holon_skill_invoke`). Finding: `OBSERVE_HOLON_DENY` omits it (`crates/operant-os/src/policy.rs:100-107`), and native tools are allowed by default (`policy.rs:181-182`). The kernel appends a `skill` event under `write_txn` (`holon/execution/service.py:1102-1103`). Read.
- [x] **OS-5 (H) — a failed chair notification exits 0, and the kernel does not retry it.** Fixed: the exit code in iter-963, the retry in Holon `992b08f` (AD-008). `notify()` moves `notify_seq` only past a delivered message; test `a_failed_delivery_is_owed_again_on_the_next_heartbeat` (Holon `tests/test_loops.py`). The Holon commit is local to that repo and not pushed. Partly fixed iter-963: `chair notify` exits nonzero when no chair was reached (`cmd_org.rs` `deliver_to_chairs`; tests `notify_is_an_error_when_every_send_fails`, `notify_succeeds_when_one_send_lands`). Still open: the retry, which is a Holon-repo change (TODO.md Review). Finding: `chair notify` prints "N sent, M failed" and returns `Ok(())` (`crates/operant-cli/src/cmd_org.rs:367-368`). The kernel advances `notify_seq` before delivery (`holon/execution/service.py:1858`). Read. Dedupe: new.
- [x] **OS-6 (H) — killing the supervisor leaves the body running.** Fixed iter-967 (AD-006): `shutdown_signal()` races the whole supervise loop (`cmd_org.rs`), and dropping the body's future drops `BodyGroup`, which SIGKILLs the body's process group (test `dropping_a_body_kills_its_whole_group`, red with the guard disarmed). A SIGKILLed supervisor is not covered; see AD-006. The body is in its own process group (`supervisor.rs:84`), and there is no signal handler in `operant-os` or `cmd_org.rs`. `kill_on_drop` does not run on signal death, so Ctrl-C or SIGTERM on `supervise` leaves the body alive until its 3600 s timeout, while its lease expires and the kernel re-claims the stage. Read; grep found no handler. Dedupe: O-12 covers group kill on timeout only.
- [x] **OS-7 (M) — every non-`running` status counts as a clean stop.** Fixed iter-967 (AD-006): `closed_by` reads the kernel status. Only `succeeded` and `failed` are a clean self-submit; `timeout`, `abandoned` and any unknown status kill the body and skip `finish` (test `only_a_self_submit_is_a_clean_stop`, red with `timeout` mapped to a self-submit). Heartbeat failure still only warns, by decision. `Ok(false)` sets `stopped_after_submit`, and the exit path maps it to 0 and skips `finish` (`supervisor.rs:146-155, 169, 192-193`). A reclaimed or cancelled run therefore looks like a successful self-submit. Heartbeat failure only warns (`:156-161`). Read.
- [x] **OS-8 (M) — a timed-out native tool leaves its kernel child running.** Fixed iter-967: the native kernel command has `kill_on_drop(true)`, so a timed-out call's child dies with its future. Every JSON key must be a plain flag name (`is_flag_name`; test `only_plain_names_become_flags`, red when every name is accepted). The timeout kill itself is not unit-tested: it needs a 120 s kernel call, so it rests on tokio's `kill_on_drop`. `tools.rs:257-261` sets no `kill_on_drop`, and the 120 s timeout wraps `wait_with_output`. The model's retry can race two `submit` processes. Every JSON key becomes a `--key` argument unchecked (`tools.rs:193-204`). Read. Dedupe: O-04 (`AUDIT-2026-10-15.md:122`) covers argument names and O-09 covers channel-named files; neither covers the timeout or retry race.
- [x] **OS-9 (L) — the idle back-off never resets after one error.** Fixed iter-967: `errored` is declared per pass in `cmd_org.rs`. No unit test: the loop talks to the kernel and has no seam, so the fix was checked by reading the loop. `errored` is set and never cleared (`crates/operant-cli/src/cmd_org.rs:502, 529, 547`), so one transient error keeps `supervise --loop` polling every 10 s instead of 30 s. Read.
- [x] **OS-10 (L) — the timeout kill reaches only the group's SIGTERM and the leader's SIGKILL.** Fixed iter-967 (AD-006): `kill_process_group` sends SIGTERM to the group, waits `KILL_GRACE`, then SIGKILLs the group. Test `a_term_trapping_grandchild_dies_with_its_group` (red without the group SIGKILL; bounded at 5 s so a regression fails fast). `kill_group_signal` sends SIGTERM to the group (`supervisor.rs:273-285`), and `child.kill()` reaches only the leader. A grandchild that traps TERM survives the timeout. Read. Dedupe: O-12 (`docs/agentic-os/AUDIT-2026-10-15.md:260-261`) covers the group kill on timeout; this row is the signal escalation that O-12 does not describe.
- [x] **OS-11 (L, latent) — Holon's driver interpolates some placeholders unquoted into `sh -c`.** Fixed in Holon `992b08f` (AD-008): `render()` refuses an `employee_slug` or `scope` that is not a plain token (`_PLAIN_TOKEN`; test `test_a_driver_refuses_a_scope_name_that_is_not_a_plain_token`). `holon/execution/drivers.py:142-143` quotes `stage`, `duty`, `role` and `posture` but not `employee_slug` or `scope`. The live `operant.toml` does not use them. Read.

- [x] **GW-1 (H) — the budget is charged to whoever is bound at write time, and it never rolls.** Fixed iter-968 (AD-009): spend is metered per turn into `seat_spend`, charged to the start-time seat, and the window reads that ledger by `recorded_at`. Tests `a_rebind_does_not_move_spend_and_the_window_rolls` (red with the window bound dropped) and `wave4_employee_window_usage_rolls_up_the_accumulator`. The ledger starts empty on upgrade (AD-009). The window sums `total_tokens` over rows whose current `employee_id` matches (`crates/operant-core/src/gateway_session.rs:1218-1224`). `total_tokens` is lifetime, and the bind at write time decides the employee (`crates/operant-core/src/gateway_session.rs:1333-1334`, `:848`, `:875`). Rebinding moves spend off the old employee, and a turn that started under X and drained after `/session Y` is charged to Y (`crates/operant-cli/src/gateway_runner.rs:1531`). Live check, read-only: chief-of-staff has 3 rows updated since 2026-10-14 totalling 8,232,833 tokens, of which 4,111,430 are on rows created before 2026-10-14.
- [x] **GW-2 (H) — cron and socialization turns use the gateway's memory bank and never set a scope.** Fixed iter-968 (AD-010): `bind_seat_run` sets `MemoryScope::Seat` on the shared agent. Test `a_seat_run_sets_its_own_seat_as_the_memory_scope` (red with the identity blanked). `bind_seat_run` sets the session, the seat and the charter, but not the scope (`crates/operant-core/src/cronjobs/scheduler.rs:478-481`). The cron agent shares the gateway's `MemoryWireProvider` (`gateway_runner.rs:2189, 2318-2320`), and the only `set_scope` callers are in the chat turn (`:1265-1273`). A cron run reads and writes whatever bank the last chat turn left. Read.
- [x] **GW-3 (H) — an unbound turn after `/new` keeps the previous employee's seat.** Fixed iter-965: `OperantAgent::clear_seat_id` runs at the start of every turn, before the binding lookup (`gateway_runner.rs`; test `unbound_turn_after_a_seated_one_runs_unseated`, red on a no-op clear). Limits: the test covers the accessor, not the consult in `stream.rs:1106`. The memory scope is still not reset when no session row matches (the `else` runs only for a found, unbound row); that is KB-8's area. Finding: the bound branch calls `set_seat_id`, and the `else` branch only resets the scope to Personal (`crates/operant-cli/src/gateway_runner.rs:1254-1276`). `/new` writes `default_employee_binding()`, which is `NULL` (`gateway_session.rs:1046`, defined at `:450`). The next turn runs with Personal scope, but the seat is still the old employee's, so every consult uses that employee's policy and the budget gate is skipped. Read. Dedupe: none.
- [x] **GW-4 (H, timing-dependent) — async memory writes resolve the bank when the worker runs.** Fixed iter-962: the enqueue-time bank from KB-7 covers this path too (test `queued_turn_keeps_the_bank_it_was_queued_under`). Finding: `SyncJob::SyncTurn` carries only the text (`crates/operant-core/src/memory_provider.rs:94-101`). `retain_turn` reads `self.bank` when the worker dequeues it (`memory_wire.rs:190-204`), which can be after the turn lock is released and another chat has set Personal. A turn can then be retained into the owner's bank. Read; the window was not reproduced.
- [x] **GW-5 (M) — `/new` reports a cleared conversation and clears nothing.** Fixed iter-968 (AD-010): `/new` closes the row by its session key (it passed the conversation id before), then persists a fresh conversation id, so the transcript and the summary are not reloaded. Store test `a_conversation_id_survives_a_save_of_its_row`; the command path has no unit seam. The derived session id depends only on the chat key (`gateway_runner.rs:464-469`), so without a `/resume` pin the same transcript and summary come back. `close_session` also runs on `session_id` and `DELETE`s by `session_key` (`gateway_session.rs:1401-1404`), which matches nothing unless the two are equal. Read.
- [x] **GW-6 (M) — a rebind keeps the chat's transcript.** Fixed iter-968 (AD-010): a seat change starts a fresh conversation, and the id persists on the row so a restart keeps it. The old transcript stays reachable by `/resume <id>`. `/resume` persists its id too. The binding changes the seat and the bank, but the in-memory conversation is keyed by session id (`gateway_runner.rs:1111`), so employee Y's turn runs with X's full transcript, and after a restart with X's last 20 rows. The summary is keyed by employee and does not leak. AD-001 and AD-004 do not cover the transcript. Read.
- [x] **GW-7 (M) — the continuation summary is keyed by chat, so `/resume` can inject the wrong roll.** Fixed iter-968 (AD-010): the continuation summary is keyed by the conversation id, so a `/resume` onto another transcript reads none of its roll. `valid_summary` and `upsert_summary` key on `session_key` (`gateway_runner.rs:1313, 1601, 1632`), while the transcript reloads by `session_id`, which a pin replaces. A `/resume` onto another transcript injects the previous topic's summary within its TTL. Read.
- [x] **GW-8 (M) — the budget gate fails open when the usage read errors.** Fixed iter-968 (AD-009): a hard cap whose usage read errors refuses the turn or the run, and soft mode runs. Tests `budget_gate_refuses_a_hard_cap_whose_spend_cannot_be_read` (red with the hard refusal disabled) and `budget_gate_soft_mode_still_runs_when_the_meter_is_unreadable`. The runner side has no unit seam. The seat-override read is a residual (AD-009). `employee_window_usage(...).ok()` returns `None`, which the gate treats as "no budget" (`gateway_runner.rs:1363-1370`). Cron does the same (`scheduler.rs:436, 452-453`). A locked database therefore runs the turn with no cap. Read.
- [x] **GW-9 (M) — `/session` reports `premiere` for an unbound chat.** Fixed iter-968 (AD-010): `/session` says an unbound chat is unbound and ungoverned. Test `an_unbound_chat_is_never_reported_as_the_premiere` (red with `premiere` in the text). The no-argument arm falls back to the string `premiere` (`crates/operant-cli/src/gateway_commands.rs:835`), and the runner treats the same row as the unbound personal seat. The no-entry text promises a binding to `premiere` as well. Dedupe: G-1 in the 2026-10-19 plan records the same defect and its option (c).

- [x] **TUI-1 (M) — a pending approval prompt is painted under later overlays, and its keys go to whichever overlay is open.** The permission gate runs after the session-browser and agents-menu gates (`crates/operant-cli/src/tui/app/key_handling.rs:677, 892, 958`), but the dialog paints before most overlays (`render/operant_overlays.rs:211`, against `:355` for the browser). With `/resume` open during a `WaitingForApproval` turn, the browser covers the prompt and drops `y`, and the turn waits until Esc. No approval bypass was found, because the browser swallows the keys. Dedupe: the ordering divergence is known and deferred (`key_handling.rs:73-92`, `docs/ROADMAP-TUI-FLEET.md` §6.1); the stalled-approval consequence is new. Read in code; not run. Fixed iter-969: the approval gate runs right after the bypass gate, before every overlay (AD-011; test `a_pending_approval_takes_the_key_before_an_open_browser`, mutation fails it).
- [x] **TUI-2 (H) — `/resume` shows another session's transcript, but the agent keeps the previous session id and context.** Fixed iter-966: `/resume` now sets `pending_switch_session`, so the agent is retargeted by the branch-switch path (`set_session_id`, then the transcript is rebuilt from the agent). The separate load channel is removed, and the header title resets on a switch. Tests: `resume_drain_retargets_the_agent_to_the_picked_session` (red when the retarget is removed) and `resume_picks_the_session_the_agent_switches_to`. Finding: `drain_session_load` replaces `app.messages` and sets "Session loaded." (`crates/operant-cli/src/tui/app/channel_drains.rs:236-270`), and never calls `set_session_id`. The only retarget is `adapter_types/tui_app.rs:1082`, which the trace places on the branch-switch path (`app/key_handling.rs:634`), not the load path. The next prompt is therefore written to, and answered from, the old session, and the header title is not reset. The `set_session_id` comment at `app/mod.rs:237` says the drain retargets, which is stale. Dedupe: new. The drain is read in code; the branch-path routing is the lane's trace, not re-run.
- [x] **TUI-3 (H) — creating an agent overwrites an existing definition with the same slug, and follows a symlink at that path.** Fixed iter-964: a new agent is refused when its path exists, a dangling symlink included, and the error names the file (`tui/agents_view.rs` `save_editor`; test `creating_an_agent_refuses_to_overwrite_an_existing_file`). Finding: With no original index, `save_editor` picks `.operant/agents/<slug>.md` and writes unconditionally (`crates/operant-cli/src/tui/agents_view.rs:317-327`, write at `:507`). `slugify_agent_name` lowercases and maps space, `-`, `_` and `.` to one `-` (`:443-460`), so "Code Reviewer", "code-reviewer" and "code_reviewer" all resolve to one file. Path traversal is not possible: the slug keeps only ASCII alphanumerics and `-`. Dedupe: new. Read in code; not run.
- [x] **TUI-4 (H) — a multi-line agent description is written unquoted and reads back as its first line.** Fixed iter-964: the description is written as a JSON-quoted YAML scalar and read back with serde_json (`tui/agents_view.rs` `serialize_editor`, `extract_yaml_str`; test `multi_line_description_round_trips_through_save_and_load`). Finding: `description: {}` is written with the raw newlines (`agents_view.rs:492`), and `extract_yaml_str` returns only the line that starts with `description:` (`:420-425`). The rest of the description is lost on the next save. Dedupe: new. Read in code; the round trip was not run.
- [x] **TUI-5 (M) — `/rewind <n>` keeps its y/n confirmation armed after any other key.** Fixed iter-964: any key other than y or n cancels the confirmation before the key is handled (`tui/app/key_handling.rs`; test `any_key_other_than_y_or_n_cancels_the_rewind_confirmation`). Finding: The `y` arm (`crates/operant-cli/src/tui/app/key_handling.rs:1166`) and the `n` arm (`:1187`) are the only handlers for `pending_rewind_confirm`, with no else branch, so the confirmation stays set while other keys pass through. A later `y` (for example inside a typed word) sets `pending_rewind` and forks the conversation. The old path is kept, so nothing is lost. Dedupe: new. Read in code; not run.
- [x] **TUI-6 (H) — a shell-out that fails to spawn leaves the terminal in cooked mode on the main screen.** Fix applied iter-966 (the spawn result is held until the terminal is restored, `tui_app.rs` `run_suspended_shell_command`). Not closed: no test forces a spawn failure, because the function needs a terminal. Raw mode is turned off and the alternate screen is left first (`crates/operant-cli/src/tui/adapter_types/tui_app.rs:1255-1256`). `cmd.spawn()?` (`:1267`) returns before the restore at `:1271-1272`, so a spawn error leaves echo on and the TUI unrendered. The lane's trigger (`operant update` replacing the binary, so `/org` gets ENOENT) is an inference, not reproduced. Dedupe: new. Read in code; not run. Fixed iter-969: the restore runs before the child's result is turned into an error (AD-014; test `the_terminal_is_restored_even_when_the_child_cannot_spawn`, mutation fails it).
- [x] **TUI-7 (M) — the shell-out prints "Returned to operant." after a non-zero exit or a kill.** `run_suspended_shell_command` returns `Ok(status)` (`tui_app.rs:1280`), and both callers discard the status (`:341-343`, `:367-370`). The status line therefore reads "Returned to operant." when `operant org holon tick` exits 1. Restoration is correct on those paths; only the message is wrong. Dedupe: new. Read in code; not run. Fixed iter-969: `shell_return_message` reports a non-zero exit or a signal (AD-014; test `a_failed_or_killed_child_is_not_reported_as_a_clean_return`).
- [x] **TUI-8 (M) — `/rename` never enters rename mode, drops its argument, and is not saved.** `/rename` calls `session_browser.open(vec![])` and then `start_rename()` (`crates/operant-cli/src/tui/app/commands.rs:1003-1008`), so no row is selected and the argument is dropped. `confirm_rename` edits only the in-memory `self.sessions[i].title` (`session_browser.rs:120-135`), and nothing writes the title to the database, so the old name returns on the next `/resume`. Dedupe: new. Read in code; not run. Fixed iter-969: the confirmed rename is saved with `update_session_title`, and a bare `/rename` starts once the list loads (AD-012; test `a_bare_rename_starts_on_the_first_row_once_the_list_arrives`).
- [x] **TUI-9 (M) — `/agents` and the HUD show a delegation as "running" when its completion never arrives, and label rows by tool-call id.** The entry is pushed keyed by `tool_call_id` (`crates/operant-cli/src/tui/app/agent_events.rs:159-161`), and completion updates only inside the `tool_use_blocks` lookup (`:190-200`). `tool_use_blocks.clear()` runs on `/new` and `/rewind` (`adapter_types/tui_app.rs:1013, 1035, 1055, 1086, 1112`), so a delegation still running then never completes its entry. The HUD takes `.first()` (`app/tui_state_impl.rs:448-452`), so the stale entry hides later ones. Not checked: whether the core emits a completion for a cancelled delegate. Dedupe: new. Read in code; not run. Fixed iter-969: a cleared delegation is marked abandoned; completion is keyed by id; the HUD shows a running delegation first (AD-013; test `a_cleared_delegation_is_abandoned_not_left_running`, mutation fails it).
- [x] **TUI-10 (M) — the shell-out leaves mouse capture on for the child.** `run_suspended_shell_command` toggles raw mode and the alternate screen but not mouse capture (`tui_app.rs:1240-1280`). Capture is enabled at `:159` and disabled only on normal exit (`:439`). A child such as the `/setup` wizard receives mouse escape sequences on stdin. The lane also reports that Ctrl-C in that child takes the TUI down with it, which is inferred and not run. Dedupe: new; the existing Ctrl-C rows (around lines 588-596) cover a different handler. Fixed iter-969: mouse capture is released for the child and restored after it (AD-014). No runtime test: the toggle is a terminal write, so a live run on a terminal is the only confirmation. Live check iter-978 (pty, installed `~/.local/bin/operant` 0.2.1 at 53f9bb7a): mouse capture is on at startup, off when `/setup` suspends the TUI, and on again after Ctrl-C ends the child, and the TUI stayed alive. The 'Ctrl-C takes the TUI down' note did not reproduce in raw mode.
- [x] **TUI-11 (M) — some TUI actions run blocking I/O on the event loop.** `session_parent` and `child_sessions` run inline (`tui_app.rs:919, 930`) under `if let Ok`, so a database error shows an empty branch list with no message. The store's connection sits behind one mutex (`crates/operant-core/src/database.rs:124`, `busy_timeout=500` at `:159`), so a long agent write blocks Ctrl+B and `/branch`. `/diff`, `/review`, `/changes` and `/rollback` run `git diff` on the loop (`diff_viewer/state.rs:31-33, 100-108`, `parse.rs:9`). The session list already uses `spawn_blocking` (`adapter_types/history.rs:21-30`). Magnitude not measured. Dedupe: new. Read in code. Partly fixed iter-969: the branch half is off the event loop and reports errors (AD-015). The git-diff load still runs on the event loop (`diff_viewer/state.rs:108`); the row stays open for it. Fixed iter-973 (AD-026): the git diff is read on a worker thread and taken on the tick, with a reading state; the branch half is AD-015.
- [x] **TUI-12 (M) — five `REGISTERED_COMMANDS` descriptions say something the handler does not do.** `/init` says "Write an AGENTS.md" but runs `setup provider` (`crates/operant-cli/src/tui/operant_app/app.rs:295`, `app/commands.rs:1020-1022`). `/verbose` says "Toggle verbose reasoning display" but cycles the output style (`app.rs:321`, `commands.rs:924-932`). `/diff` and `/review` open the git diff viewer (`app.rs:256, 200`, `commands.rs:344-348`). `/rollback` says "Alias of /rewind" but only opens the last-turn diff, and nothing is rewound (`app.rs:308`, `commands.rs:1211-1217`). Dedupe: new. Read in code; not run. Fixed iter-969: the registered descriptions of /keys, /diff, /review, /reload, /update, /branch, /changes, /init, /mouse, /rollback, /subgoal and /verbose match their handlers.
- [x] **TUI-13 (L) — further descriptions drift from their handlers.** `/mouse` prints a literal "enabled" under `--no-mouse` (`app.rs:298`, `commands.rs:1347-1351`). `/reload` reloads settings only (`app.rs:259`, `commands.rs:1235-1237`). `/update` prints an instruction and does not update (`app.rs:260`, `commands.rs:1382-1388`). `/keys` ignores `refresh` (`app.rs:252-253`, `commands.rs:1048-1066`). `/branch` opens the fork-tree overlay and is not an alias of `/resume` (`app.rs:281`, `commands.rs:511-517`). `/changes` shows the turn diff, not the working tree (`app.rs:283`, `commands.rs:349-353`). `/subgoal <text>` sets the goal (`app.rs:317`, `commands.rs:703`). Dedupe: new. The descriptions for `/reload`, `/mouse`, `/branch`, `/init`, `/verbose`, `/review` and `/rollback` were re-read in this pass; the handler lines for the rest are the lane's, not re-run. Fixed iter-969: `/mouse` reports the live capture state, not a literal string.

## OPEN — Red-team: knowledge-base surface (2026-10-19)

Found by a read-only red-team of the knowledge-base tools (`learning_manage`, `skill_manage` and `skill_view`, `memory_store`, `memory_search`, the memory-wire provider, and the plugin memory path), then checked against the code. The reachability table is in `docs/kb/workflows/knowledge-base-redteam-2026-10-19.md`. KB-n is finding n of that record.

- [x] **KB-1 (H) — `learning_manage` delete and edit follow a `node_id` out of the skills dir.** `delete_skill_node` joined the raw id onto `skills_dir`, so `../../.ssh` renamed `~/.ssh` into `~/.operant/.ssh` and returned `ok: true`. `learning_manage` is not in `is_permission_gated_tool`, so no prompt appeared. Critical in the red-team. Fixed iter-958: `node_detail`, `delete_node`, and `edit_node` refuse any skill id that is not plain path segments (`is_skill_node_path`, `crates/operant-core/src/agent/learning_graph.rs`). Regression test `test_skill_node_id_cannot_escape_skills_dir`.

- [x] **KB-2 (H) — `skill_manage` joins `name` and `category` without a containment check.** `find_skill` joined the raw name, so `write_file` with `name: "../../../x"` overwrote a file under any directory that holds a `SKILL.md`. `create` validated `name` but not `category`. Fixed iter-958: `find_skill` refuses a name that is not plain path segments (`is_plain_relative_path`), and `create` runs `validate_name` on `category` (`crates/operant-core/src/tools/skills_tool.rs`). Regression test `test_find_skill_refuses_name_outside_skills_dir`. `resolve_skill_target` is still lexical only, see KB-16.

## OPEN — Red-team survey after iter-941 (2026-10-19)

Each row is a finding from `docs/plans/2026-10-19-remaining-implementation-gaps-after-iter-941-red-team-survey.md`. The plan holds the evidence and the phase that closes each row. M = user-visible or safety-relevant; L = hygiene or drift.

- [x] **RT-1 (M, premise corrected iter-942; Phase 2 iter-943) — 136 of 701 operant-cli config fields have no file source at all.** `CliConfig::load()` (`operant-cli/src/config.rs:1649`) reads only `.env` and `HERMES_*` env vars. It never reads `config.yaml` or `config.local.yaml` (`config_file` and `local_config_file` are set and never read). `operant.toml` rejects these sections: `[display]` fails with `unknown field display` (reproduced on the iter-941 binary). So the fields keep their defaults and cannot be set by a user. Phase 2 deletes them, unless a field has a reader and a file path. Sampled dead: `show_cost`, `persistent_output`, `bell_on_complete`, `inline_diffs`, `title_generation`, `docker_image`. Phase 2 (iter-943): 167 `pub` field definitions removed from `operant-cli/src/config.rs` (398 → 231), with their initializers, and 12 structs that were left with no fields or were never constructed (63 → 51). No code reads a removed field, no `HERMES_<field>` variable names one, and no code serializes `CliConfig`, so runtime behavior is unchanged. Not done: fields whose name also occurs elsewhere in the code (the name scan counted those as reads), and fields rustc sees as read only through the derived `Serialize` impl.
- [x] **RT-2 — CLOSED AS NOT REPRODUCING (iter-942).** The documented config already rejects unknown keys: `operant.toml` with `[agent] modle = …` exits 1 with `unknown field modle` (reproduced on the iter-941 binary). The survey's `config.rs:1070` note concerns the operant-cli structs, which are never read from a file (RT-1).
- [x] **RT-3 (M) — `[keybindings]` overrides are not loaded** (TODO.md Pending). The registry, `/keys`, and the conflict report exist. Phase 3. Survey, iter-944: loading overrides into the registry would change only `/keys`, `/hotkeys`, and the usage counter. Dispatch is the if-chain in `operant-cli/src/tui/app/key_handling.rs`, and `operant-cli/src/tui/keybindings.rs:7-11` records that migrating dispatch to the registry is a separate, riskier change. A `[keybindings]` table that the dispatcher ignores would be a false feature, so no loader is added yet. Needed: a dispatch migration (registry-driven match, with each action keeping its current key), then the loader. `DEFAULT_KEYBINDINGS` is a static `LazyLock`, so the loader needs its own registry instance. Fixed iter-973 (AD-024): `[keybindings]` is accepted and warned about, so it no longer fails the config load. The override loader still waits for the dispatch migration.
- [x] **RT-4 — FIXED iter-942.** `init_logging` returns `Result`; a log file that cannot be opened is a startup error naming the path (`main.rs`), not a panic. The `#[expect]` that covered the old `expect` is removed.
- [x] **RT-5 (M) — the `#[expect]` reason "invariant guaranteed by surrounding validation" appears 96 times, and the gate does not check the text.** Phase 4.1. Fixed iter-952. The iter-951 per-crate split was wrong: the 96 sites are operant-core 52, operant-cli 20, operant-tools 11, operant-providers 9, operant-config 1, operant-memory 1, operant-api 1, operant-macros 1. Each reason now names the invariant its guarded code relies on. 23 sites are test fixtures (`client.rs`, `providers/lib.rs`) and say so. Six attributes were removed because their call now returns an error instead: `cmd_backup` create and list, `cmd_service` log tail, `start_gateway`'s session-store path, and `ToolResult::success` and `success_with_name` (see RT-21). One group was not an invariant: the token-to-header `expect`s in the Home Assistant, Spotify, and LinkedIn tools, now fallible and fixed in iter-955 (RT-20). The PKCE non-Unix fallback was the other, fixed in iter-953 (RT-19). The gate still does not check reason text; that is a review convention, not a lint.
- [x] **RT-6 — FIXED (iter-942, iter-944, iter-986).** The `set_var` writes in the auth commands are gone, and `main` builds the tokio runtime after `CliConfig::load()`, so the `.env` load runs before any worker exists (iter-942). **RT-6b, startup: FIXED iter-944.** `[proxy]` is now part of `AppConfig` (`operant-core/src/config.rs`), which rejected it before. `apply_startup_proxy` runs in `main` before the tokio runtime is built: it sets the runtime proxy config that the HTTP clients read, and with `scope = "environment"` it exports `HTTP_PROXY`/`HTTPS_PROXY`/`NO_PROXY` before any worker exists. Regression: `proxy_table_parses_from_operant_toml`. **RT-6b, residual closed iter-949:** the unregistered `proxy_config` agent tool is deleted (`operant-tools/src/proxy_config.rs`). It had no production constructor, and its writes went to `config.toml`, which nothing reads. Its runtime `apply_to_process_env` call went with it. Proxy settings are edited in `operant.toml` `[proxy]`, applied at startup. **RT-6c: FIXED iter-986 (AD-038).** The row described a residual RACE: "a real rotation still writes the process env while the runtime is live, and a C-level `getenv` in a child can race that write." Reading the code found something worse: the rotation did not work AT ALL. `reload_dotenv` wrote `OPENAI_API_KEY` into the process environment, and NOTHING in the running agent read it back. The client captured its key when it was constructed, the credential pool seeded its keys at startup, and the gateway never called `apply_env_overrides` per turn. The feature the module's own doc comment claims — "enabling credential rotation without restarting the long-lived gateway daemon" — was a no-op. Four defects, in the order they were found. (1) The rotation never reached the pool: `PooledModelClient`'s request loop calls `pool.select()` and then `inner.set_api_key(&cred.value)`, so a key set only on the inner client was replaced by the pool's startup-seeded value on the next turn. (2) The pool's credential could not be found by source: `add_seed` DEDUPES BY VALUE and `apply_env_overrides` copies `OPENAI_API_KEY` into `config.client.api_key`, so the `env:`-seeded credential is dropped and the pool holds ONE credential whose source is the config seed and whose VALUE came from the environment. Adding a new credential would put it behind the stale primary under `FillFirst`. (3) The reload read the WRONG FILE: a CWD-relative `.env`, while the startup loader reads `<HERMES_HOME or ~/.operant>/.env` — so in the documented setup the rotation watched a file nobody else does. (4) "Use this key now" and "replace the stored secret" had been conflated in one method, which would corrupt the pool on every 401 and every 429. Fixed in four parts. (a) `ModelClient` gained `rotate_credential_value` ("replace the stored secret"), separate from `set_api_key` ("use this key now"); `OperantAgent::set_api_key` calls the former. (b) `PooledModelClient` tracks the credential it last SELECTED and replaces that one's value in place (new `CredentialPool::replace_value`); it no-ops when the pool already holds the value (the agent-loop rotation path) and never adds a credential. (c) `dotenv_path()` resolves `OPERANT_ENV_FILE` → `HERMES_ENV_FILE` → `<operant_home>/.env` → `.env` in the CWD, so the reload reads the file the startup loader reads. (d) A base-URL retarget happens only on a CHANGE and keeps the key the client is currently using, because `set_endpoint` with no key empties a pooled client's credentials. The process-environment write is kept: tools and child processes read the environment. Tests: nine in `agent::pooled_client` and three in `env_passthrough`, including the production pool shape (one credential named "primary", source "config (client.api_key)", holding the environment's value) and the full `try_rotate_credential` sequence. Two mutations were run: `rotate_credential_value` not touching the pool fails 5 tests, and `set_api_key` also rewriting the pool fails `the_agent_loop_rotation_does_not_rewrite_the_benched_credential` — the first attempt at that mutation was NOT caught, which is why that test exists. The residual C-level `getenv` race in a child process remains: closing it needs an explicit child environment across the ~206 `Command::new` sites. **iter-985's published claim was false and is corrected in iter-986**: it said the rotation took effect on the next turn, and it did not — the fix landed in iter-985 did not reach the pool and read the wrong file.
- [x] **RT-7 — FIXED iter-942.** `crates/operant-providers/src/glm.rs` deleted (no `mod` declaration, no caller; in history), with its stale `lib.rs` comment.
- [x] **RT-8 (L) — stray untracked `xd:/` directory at the repo root** (`report_issue`, 2026-10-16, a todo-tool note). Phase 4.5. Fixed iter-970: `xd:/` (a 220-byte note from a todo-tool bug) is excluded in `.git/info/exclude`; the file stays on disk because it is the owner's.
- [x] **RT-9 (L) — tracked `Cargo.lock` is dirty in the shared tree** (memory-wire sync, six `windows-sys` lines). Phase 4.5. Decision iter-970: `Cargo.lock` stays tracked. The dirty `windows-sys`/`getrandom` lines come from the memory-wire sync in `scripts/build.sh`, so they are committed with the release iteration that builds from them, not left in the tree. Closed iter-975: `Cargo.lock` is committed with the release (the memory-wire git dependency advanced `20e71a3` → `12fc2db`, the sha the binary reports).
- [x] **RT-10 (M) — `deny.toml` exists, and no workflow or script runs `cargo deny` or `cargo audit`.** Phase 4.2. Iter-944: `cargo audit` run locally against the advisory database reported 21 vulnerabilities. The lock bump (`extism` 1.41.0, which brings `wasmtime` 49.0.3; `rustls` 0.23.46; `quinn-proto` 0.11.19; `h2` 0.4.20; `aws-lc-sys` 0.46) and `pdf-extract` 0.12 (`lopdf` 0.42.0) clear 18 of them. Still open: three `zip` advisories (RT-18). `cargo deny` is not installed here, and `cargo audit` is not gated in CI. Fixed iter-982: `scripts/supply-chain-gate.sh` runs `cargo audit` against the committed lockfile and fails on any advisory the committed allowlist (`.ci/advisory-allowlist.txt`) does not cover — the same delta-against-a-committed-list shape as the clippy gate. Wired into `scripts/self-test.sh`. The allowlist is not a blanket exemption: each of its 3 entries names a specific advisory and carries the RT-26/RT-27/RT-28 reason. Current state: 0 vulnerabilities, 3 unmaintained advisories, all 3 allowlisted. `cargo deny` stays uninstalled on purpose — a gate nobody can pass is worse than no gate, which `deny.toml`'s own header already argues — so the licence and duplicate-version policies remain unexecuted policy. Regression: `scripts/tests/supply_chain_gate_test.sh` stubs cargo-audit and asserts the gate FAILS on an uncovered advisory, passes on a covered one, reports a stale entry, and fails on a missing allowlist. The clippy gate had the same `set -e` bug (an all-comment allowlist made `grep -v` exit 1 and abort the script); fixed in the same commit.
- [x] **RT-11 (M) — the corpus validator is red (21 errors) and not gated.** Phase 4.3. Fixed iter-951. Of the 21 errors, 14 were validator drift: `SNAP_TOP` was a stale transcription (now read from `debug_snapshot()` source), `NAMED` lacked the four page keys the Rust parser accepts, and `assert_screen` rejected the `count:NEEDLE==N` grammar the runner implements. Five were `deterministic: true` (a no-op: the runner acts only on `is False`), now removed. One was an empty `no-events.json` for the pending-turn scenario; an empty script is now a valid fixture and is exempt from the terminal-event rule. The W008 rule fires on a heading-NAME match, and every one of the four was a coincidental name collision on a different topic: the README's "Requirements" is build prerequisites while CONTRIBUTING's is test requirements; the README's "License" is the project's licensing while CONTRIBUTING's is the contributor agreement; the README's "Configuration" is operant.toml while kernel.md's is the kernel's own table; the README's "How it works" is the agent loop while the TUI spec's is the headless simulator. Renaming is the honest fix, not a paraphrase removal: the README's content is the operator-facing answer to each question and is correct where it stands. No workspace crate calls zip's extract API (checked iter-944), so the advisory is not reached today. Clearing it needs a `kokoro-tiny` release that moves off `ndarray-npy` 0.9, or a decision to drop TTS from the default features. `deny.toml` keeps no ignore list by policy, so the advisory stays visible until then. The workspace now patches `ndarray-npy` 0.9.1 to `zip` 9 (`vendor/ndarray-npy`, README there). `cargo audit --no-fetch` lists no `zip` advisory, the vendored crate passes 39 tests, and the live check `kokoro_speaks_through_the_tool_with_the_cached_model` passes (1.25 s at 24 kHz, peak 17082).
- [x] **RT-19 (M) — the PKCE code verifier falls back to a time-seeded pseudo-random state on non-Unix targets.** Fixed iter-953. `generate_code_verifier` (`operant-core/src/mcp_oauth.rs`) now draws 64 bytes from `rand::thread_rng()`, a CSPRNG seeded by the OS on every target. The `/dev/urandom` read and its two `expect`s are gone with the fallback. `rand = { workspace = true }` is added to `operant-core`, which adds one `rand 0.8.7` entry to its `Cargo.lock` block. Verified: `operant-core` lib tests 2305 passed, 0 failed; clippy gate `seen=52 allowlist=52 new=0 stale=0`.
- [x] **RT-20 (M) — a bearer token that is not header-safe panics the tool call.** `HeaderValue::from_str` rejects control characters and non-ASCII. Four Home Assistant call sites (`tools/home_assistant_tool.rs` `get_headers`), four Spotify call sites (`tools/spotify_tool.rs` `headers`), and the LinkedIn `api_headers` and `upload_image` paths all `expect` it. A token with a stray control character from config crashes the tool call instead of returning an error. Fixed iter-955: the Home Assistant `get_headers` and Spotify `headers` builders return `Result`, and each caller returns `ToolResult::error`. The LinkedIn `api_headers` returns `anyhow::Result`; its request path and 401 retry propagate it, and `upload_image` maps it with `context`. Regression tests: `test_get_headers_rejects_token_with_control_character` and `api_headers_rejects_token_with_control_character`. The Spotify error path has no direct test (its token comes from process config) and uses the same pattern. Verified: `operant-core` lib 2306 passed, `operant-tools` lib 1157 passed; clippy gate `new=0 stale=0`.
- [x] **RT-21 (M) — four runtime panics on fallible paths, found during RT-5.** Fixed iter-952, with regression tests where the path is testable. (a) `WebhookAdapter::start_with_channel` bound its listener inside a detached `tokio::spawn` with `expect`. A port conflict panicked the task while the adapter reported itself started. The bind now runs before the spawn, and its error returns to the gateway, which logs it. Test: `start_with_channel_returns_bind_error_when_port_is_taken`. (b) `ToolResult::success` and `success_with_name` are generic over `Serialize`. Content that fails to serialize (a map with non-string keys) panicked the tool call. It now returns an error result. Test: `success_reports_unserializable_content_as_an_error_result`. (c) `operant backup create` panicked when the clock was before 1970 or the working directory was gone. Both now return an error. `operant backup list` panicked on a backup directory with a pre-1970 mtime. It now shows `0`. (d) `operant service logs` and the gateway session store called `to_str().unwrap()` on the log and database paths. The log tail now passes the path as an OS string. A non-UTF-8 database path now leaves the session store off, the same way an open failure already did, so that failure is silent too.
- [x] **RT-22 (M) — `atty` in `kokoro-tiny` 0.1.0 carries RUSTSEC-2021-0145 (unsound) and RUSTSEC-2024-0375 (unmaintained).** Fixed iter-977 (AD-033). `vendor/kokoro-tiny` is the crate with `atty` removed; its only use, the binary's stdin check, now calls `std::io::IsTerminal`. A bad store entry is still skipped, not fatal.
